- **User question: is the wall guard's 4% a day good or too low; what does POOL4 use?** Checked: the guard caps the wall's *price*, not its IMD (all the reserve goes in at each rebalance, in both hooks). POOL4's CappedBurnHook source (Sourcify exact match): `floorDecayTicksPerDay` default 400, owner-set 1–2,000 ("a higher rate tracks recoveries sooner and shortens the holding period a manipulator must sustain"); **its live value on Ethereum is 2,000** (`cast call`, 2026-10-17; `maxRefStep` 200). Ours is the constant 400 with no owner setting. At our 14,000 IMD seed, 400 lets the wall's top reach ~18,500 IMD market cap after a week of rally, ~25,500 after 15 days, ~46,500 after 30 (a doubling takes 17.3 days); 1,000 → 28,200 / 62,700 / 281,000 (6.9 days); 2,000 → 56,800 / 281,000 / 5.6M (3.5 days). **Recommended: 2,000, as POOL4 runs it** (a constant, same bytecode size; tests in `test/full/Hook.t.sol`, `test/full/HookRevision.t.sol` and the Sepolia fork, README, NatSpec, the A3 job text; full suites on `next` and `mainnet`). Waiting for the user.
- **Wall guard, discussed (user: "keep it for discussion"):** the setting caps only the wall's price, never lets anyone dump; at the open it changes little (the guard starts at the seed price; sells are gated); during the game a faster guard follows rallies (one 15-day season: 1.8× at 400, 4.5× at 1,000, 20× at 2,000); after the death it sets how long a whale must hold a fake price to lift the wall (a doubling: 17 / 7 / 3.5 days; each day's climb 4% / 10.5% / 22% against a ~3.5% round-trip fee). The user leans to 1,000; recommended 1,000, fixed.
- **Trim vs wall (user asked):** two mechanisms that work together: the trim (`_applyCap`, every swap) burns PLEA sold back into the market band above the inventory cap at once and moves the same share of the band's IMD into the wall's reserve; the wall (`rebalance`) places that reserve as an IMD bid below the price, buys PLEA when the price falls into it and burns it at the next rebalance. Plus the 0.25% PLEA fee burn on every trade. The guard rate touches only the wall's placement; the trim's pace is the cap ratchet (`CAP_DECAY_DAYS` 15).
- **Owner setting for the guard rate (user's discussion item):** implementable in principle (POOL4 has `setFloorDecay`, owner, 1–2,000), but measured in a throwaway build: POOL4's style +251 B (PleaHook 24,653 B, 77 over EIP-170); a lean setter +161 B (24,563 B, 13 under; no room for a delay or any later fix). A new owner power (our docs say the owner cannot change market settings; the imd/acc panel judged owner powers). Recommended: keep it fixed. The scratch worktree was removed; nothing committed in `khaed1/plea`.
- **User (~17:00 UTC): "just make it 700" → the wall guard 400 → 700 ticks a day** (`PleaHook.WALL_GUARD_TICKS_PER_DAY`, ~7.25% a day: a doubling followed in ~10 days, up to ~2.9× in a 15-day season; fixed, no owner setting). On `next` (local `0c891ae`): the constant and its NatSpec; tests `test/full/Hook.t.sol` (`test_wallGuardDropsAtOnceAndRisesSevenPercentADay`, which also checks the constant is 700; the 8-minute bound 4), `test/full/HookRevision.t.sol` (`testFuzz_theGuardClimbsAtMost700TicksADay`; the drained-pool step bound 5), `test/fork/ForkTestnet.t.sol` (rise ≤ 701, the pump > 700 ticks); README (the guard paragraph), THREAT-MODEL invariant 21 (**700 ticks**), the A3 job text, FINDINGS "Changes after run 8.5". PleaHook still 24,402 B (174 under). `mainnet` merged locally (`bcecb90`).
- **Caught on the first suite run: 508 passed in 69 suites, not 852 in 91.** My first edit wrote a bound as `700 * 8 minutes / 1 days + 1`; Solidity evaluates literal arithmetic exactly (4.89, not an integer), so `test/full/Hook.t.sol` did not compile and its whole group (344 tests) never ran on either branch, while `run_suite.sh` summed the other groups and printed "failed 0". Fixed the bound (4; the local commits redone, nothing had been pushed), and **`plea/tools/run_suite.sh` now prints `NO RESULT (compile error or crash)` for a group without a totals line, cleans its log dir first, lists such groups at the end and exits 1.** Both suites re-running.

- **2026-10-21 ~09:00 UTC: Glamsterdam gas (user asked what it is).** Glamsterdam has been live on Sepolia since 2026-10-06 13:53 UTC; Hoodi 2026-10-26; mainnet date not set; its gas EIPs are in Last Call until 2026-11-01. EIP-8037 makes new state "state gas": a new storage slot 97,920 + 12,100 (was 22,100), a new account 183,600, code 1,530 gas a byte; EIP-8038 makes a slot update 10,100–12,100. In a transaction of at most 16,777,216 gas the state gas comes out of the gas a contract forwards to a sub-call. This is why run 9's deploys cost 34–39M gas each on Sepolia (IMD's launch tx 79.6M). **Measured on live Sepolia** (eth_call with state overrides: the Intake's code replaced by a probe calling the consumer with a fixed gas, a pending plea / objection written into storage, a test oracle signer; scratch only, nothing sent): **gate approval ~174k, a seller's first denial ~296k, a repeat denial ~198k, a first objection ruling (sustained or overruled) ~313k.** The Intake's `callbackGas()` is **1,000,000 on Sepolia** (run 9's live test unaffected) and **200,000 on mainnet**: once Glamsterdam is on mainnet, first denials and objection rulings would not be delivered (the Intake records delivered: false), the plea can only expire, and a lost denial does not reset the 33-hour clock. Cause: each callback writes new slots (`consumed[requestId]`; `lastDeniedAt[seller]` on a first denial; the Tribunal case's first slot on its first ruling). Told the user; recommended asking the dev whether the mainnet Intake's callback gas will be raised (else: callbacks without new slots, a new audit round and run). Nothing recorded in FINDINGS, no code changed.
- **User: what would changing the contracts do on mainnet?** Explained: nothing of ours is on mainnet yet (no migration); the contracts have no admin and cannot be upgraded, so this must be settled before the mainnet launch. A contract fix would reserve the callback's storage in the plea / objection transaction (the user pays ~22k gas once today, ~110k after Glamsterdam) and must replace the `consumed` replay mark (~110k of every callback) with another binding of each answer to its plea (e.g. the signed questionHash; IMD's hashing rule to be confirmed from run 9's real answers); pre-reserving only `lastDeniedAt` and the case slot is not enough (approval ~174k, ruling ~215k). Plan effects: change on `next` → main/mainnet, a review round, a Sepolia run 10, re-pin the mainnet launch job (~a week). IMD-side fixes need no change of ours: raise the mainnet `callbackGas`, or have the writer send verdict transactions with more than ~17.1M gas (measured: a first denial then lands, the state gas coming from the reservoir). **Recommended: ask the dev first; no mainnet launch until one of the two is settled.**
- **Checked: IMD can fix the callback gas without touching the Intake's code.** The Intake `0xa43e…de82` (same code on mainnet and Sepolia) has an owner-only `setCallbackGas(uint64)` (Ownable2Step; `cast selectors --resolve` on the mainnet bytecode; a call from another address reverts `OwnableUnauthorizedAccount`, a simulated call from the owner succeeds). Owner on both chains: **`0x047F606fD5b2BaA5f5C6c4aB8958E45CB6B054B7`** (the dev's wallet, an EOA; it also owns imdTEST). `callbackGas()` 200,000 on mainnet, 1,000,000 on Sepolia. Other owner setters: `setWriter`, `setSigner`, `setPayTo`, `setPrice`. The relay's gas limit is the writer's (`0x3d3C…25CB`) own choice, off chain, and only possible once Glamsterdam is on mainnet (until then Fusaka's cap rejects transactions above 16,777,216 gas). Suggested ask: mainnet `callbackGas` at least 400k (measured need up to ~313k).

- **Live finding (verified in the code and on chain, not a fix yet, put to the user):** a sale takes the PLEA out of the **buy record first** (`PleaHook._updateBasis`, sell branch: `pleaHeld -= pleaMoved`, `imdSpent` scaled), and a letter's or site plea's **fee sale is a sale in the writer's name**. After letter #4's fee sale p3's record fell from 213,295 to ~82k PLEA, so its `APPEAL` letter scored (82k×13 + 434k×10)/516k ≈ 10 profit points (fact 31, need 40) instead of the original's 11 (32, 39); after its appeal's fee sale p3's record is 0 (all its PLEA now counts as received: held since the open, profit 10). p4's record fell 213,184 → 79,355 after one fee sale. Consistent with THREAT-MODEL invariant 22 ("reduced only by that trader's own final sales; escrowed letter PLEA keeps its record"), but the bought-first order is not written down: a mixed holder at a loss drifts to profit 10 (worse), one at a large gain to profit 10 and held since the open (better).

- **Uniswap routing for hooked pools (user asked, ~15:30 UTC; Uniswap's "Aggregator Hooks" launched 2026-10-22: Uniswap Labs' own hooks that wrap other DEXs' liquidity as v4 pools, on Ethereum, Base and Robinhood Chain; nothing for PLEA to change):** Uniswap's routing (web app, Trading API) picks up many v4 hooks automatically, but per its support article a hook that uses `beforeSwapReturnsDelta` / `afterSwapReturnsDelta` (or dynamic fees, or a 0x91… address) must be **manually allowlisted**. **PleaHook uses both return-delta flags** (address flags `0b10100011001100`: beforeInitialize, beforeAddLiquidity, beforeSwap, afterSwap, beforeSwapReturnDelta, afterSwapReturnDelta; static LP fee), so without allowlisting the Uniswap app will not route PLEA. Form: https://developers.uniswap.org/hook-allowlist (name, email, Telegram, hook name/description/address, a pool ID with some liquidity per chain, checkboxes 0x91 / major pairs / none, chains, **verified source required** (a repo link alone is rejected), audits and website optional; no timeline; "solely for routing-compatibility purposes … not a security audit or an endorsement"). The docs say nothing on hooks that revert some swaps (our exact-output buys, plain sells while the Cabal lives) or on hookData (our buys fall back to the Universal Router's `msgSender()`). The alternative is running a UniswapX filler for the pool (heavy). **Mainnet to-do (not urgent):** buys through our own site first; after the launch, apply for the allowlist with the verified mainnet hook and pool; before applying, a fork check of Universal Router exact-input buys with empty hookData and of an exact-output quote.

- **Checked for the Uniswap allowlist form (user asked for a draft, given in chat, ~16:50 UTC):** (1) **Uniswap's router cannot buy PLEA while the Cabal lives, allowlisted or not:** v4-periphery `V4Router._swapExactInputSingle` (main) reads `amountOut` from the swap's BalanceDelta and reverts `V4TooLittleReceived` below `amountOutMinimum`, and our hook takes the whole PLEA output and `take`s it to the buyer (`PleaHook.afterSwap`, "Take the whole PLEA output"), so the router sees 0 (a quoter would quote 0); after the death the pool uses plain v4 accounting and routes normally. (2) **Mainnet site buys:** Universal Router `V4_SWAP` with `amountOutMinimum` 0 and hookData `abi.encode(buyer)`, then `BALANCE_CHECK_ERC20` (command 0x0e: owner, token, minBalance; `BalanceTooLow`) on the buyer's PLEA = balance before + minimum out, for slippage protection (the run 9 site buys through PoolSwapTest with no minimum: `plea/site-design/run9-site-review.md`). To rehearse on a mainnet fork before the site job. (3) **Verification:** Sourcify has CabalGate, Seasons and PleaDistributor of run 9; PLEA, PleaLaunch, PleaHook, Letterbox and Tribunal are not verified (Sourcify/Blockscout; Etherscan's page is behind Cloudflare here); Uniswap rejects unverified hooks. Pool ID (run 9) `0xa690c222671233f990738230fdb3e4e1b886bf9e67fae50ba6640624cedffc19` (imdTEST/PLEA, fee 0, tick spacing 60).

- **Aggregator research (user asked: can people buy PLEA through 1inch, 0x and others; report given in chat, ~17:05 UTC; research by a subagent from official docs and router source, the decisive points checked here in our code):** **while the Cabal lives no aggregator can buy PLEA**: delta-checking routers (0x Settler, Uniswap's V4Router, Velora through the Universal Router) see 0 PLEA because our hook `take`s the output to the buyer; own-balance routers (1inch V6, Velora Augustus, Odos, LI.FI, Bebop, CoW settlement) either fail `RecipientRequired` (no hookData, no `msgSender()`; only Uniswap's routers and 0x's Base Settler answer it) or would name themselves the buyer and then cannot pass the PLEA on (`CabalIsWatching`), so the whole transaction reverts and nothing is stranded; KyberSwap measures the receiver's balance but its hookData is static per hook. So no change to the hook (rejecting contract buyers would also block smart-contract wallets). **After the death:** plain accounting, routable once each aggregator supports the return-delta fee hook: Uniswap (allowlist form), 0x ("Custom Uniswap v4 Hook Request" form; Ethereum hooked pools enabled 2026-05-31), Velora (a PR to `supportedHooks` in paraswap-dex-lib with a hook simulator), KyberSwap (a PR to kyberswap-dex-lib, already simulates return-delta hooks); 1inch, Odos, OpenOcean, CoW (solvers), Enso by contact; wallets (MetaMask, Rabby, Coinbase) inherit their providers. **Risk to plan for:** sell simulations revert while the Cabal lives, so honeypot scanners (Blockaid behind MetaMask's and, per the research, Uniswap's warnings; GoPlus, honeypot.is) will likely flag PLEA; say it plainly on the site and X and check the scanners after the mainnet launch. Per the research, Uniswap's support article (updated 2026-10-16) also asks that a hook "must not require custom calldata" or be upgradeable and "should not modify or bypass the AMM protocol fee" (our swaps run through the AMM; the gated phase needs a named buyer).

fork trading playthrough through the site's seed control, buys, the IOU path and the Cabal's death; automated checks on every route × 320/360/768/1280 × light/dark; chain truth + code review + rebuild; two mockup-fidelity passes), each followed by a skeptic. **158 findings stand (1 refuted): 3 blocker, 17 high, 83 medium, 50 low, 5 nit**, with many cross-tester duplicates. **Blockers:** (1) right after the seed the site cannot quote the first buy, so Approve and Buy stay disabled for everyone, the owner's opening 2 imdTEST buy included (float tick-to-sqrt makes the market band look inactive at the seed price); (2) after a lapsed approval the site never lets that wallet plead again ("Plea #N is open"; its "Mark plea lapsed" button calls `CabalGate.expire`, which always reverts), and an expired unanswered plea blocks the same way. **High** includes: auto-appeal preview amount wrong (ignores the escrow the Letterbox returns first); Seasons shows the ended-but-unclosed season's pot as the next season's and hides its board; season views fall back to season 1 after the death; swap errors not decoded (PoolManager `WrappedError`; 54 of 120 ABI errors unmapped); partial fills reported as success; launch-window fee rows say 2.25%; Sell now's default minOut from the hourly checkpoint (reverts after a ~1% dip); the Letterbox's 0.70 floor / 10% fee rule hidden on the default auto-sell Plead; the need marker overflows (sideways scroll on Profile at every width); the first RPC hanging leaves the site blank ~7 min; the Seasons page and plea page miss most of their mockups. **Works:** every write decodes to the right call with gas = ceil(estimate × 1.3), fact score/need = `CabalGate.factScore`, CannotPass, the text checks, the stamp lands once, the urgent window only for the seller, share PNGs 1200 × 675, axe 0 violations in the fork runs. Results: session scratchpad `wf/final.json` (findings + verdicts), `wf/<tester>/` evidence. - **SITE NOTES FOR TODAY'S RUN 9 LIVE TEST (read before 15:00 UTC):**
  1. **Letters need about 1.44M PLEA or more.** A tester who buys ~10 imdTEST inside the 90-minute launch window gets only ~0.6M PLEA (the launch fee), and the site lets them press Send letter and then fails with wrong advice. Letter senders should buy at least ~9 imdTEST after the launch window (16:30 UTC), or skip letters on small wallets.
  2. **Untick "Sell automatically if approved"** on Plead and Appeal for small wallets: the default auto-sell box blocks Submit (the reason says so).
  3. **Keep ~1 imdTEST spare for fees:** the site does not check the fixed 0.50 fee / 0.85 objection fee + stake against the balance before the approval (Max then Submit fails after the approval).
  4. **"Nothing was sent" can be wrong:** when the public RPC throttles right after a confirmed transaction, the site may report a confirmed sale/record/delivery as failed. Check the sepolia.etherscan.io link (or the plea page after a refresh) before retrying.
  5. **An objection with no answer after 20 minutes** still reads "pending … points held" until someone presses "Expire unanswered objection" on #/objections; press it, and the case reads VOID and the points count.
  6. **Phones:** use the wallet app's own browser (MetaMask / Rabby / Coinbase / Phantom in-app browser). A phone browser that only announces a wallet through EIP-6963 gets only "Open in …" links (IMD4).
  7. Cosmetic: the runner who delivers someone's auto-sell/letter plea sees the writer's "sold for you" toast; plea/objection/letter toasts show two "Open plea #N" links; "NaN%" in How it works.
  Results: session scratchpad `wf/retest2.json`. Wallet A: 0.80 IMD (one more job). **Recommended: no fix job before 15:00** (a job takes ~2 h and would change the site mid-test); after the live test, one round-3 job with the 14 partly items, the 48 new issues, IMD4/IMD5/WW9 and whatever the live test finds.
